CITY OF MONTICELLO
WRIGHT COUNTY, MINNESOTA
PLANNING COMMISSION
RESOLUTION PC-2026-48
A RESOLUTION OF THE PLANNING COMMISSION OF THE
CITY OF MONTICELLO RECOMMENDING APPROVAL OF A
CONDITIONAL USE PERMIT FOR DATA CENTER AND DATA CENTER
CAMPUS USES IN THE EDMONSON BUSINESS CENTER DATA CENTER
PLANNED UNIT DEVELOPMENT DISTRICT
WHEREAS, the City of Monticello has received an application from Nick Frattalone (“the
Applicant”) for a Conditional Use Permit for Data Center and Data Center Campus Uses on the
proposed Lot 1 and 2, Block 1, Edmonson Business Center (“the Property”); and
WHEREAS, the Property is currently located in the Monticello Orderly Annexation Area (MOAA)
and primarily used for agricultural and agricultural residential purposes; and
WHEREAS, all property owners of the Property have also signed the land use application and
petitioned for annexation to the City of Monticello; and
WHEREAS, the Joint Resolution for Orderly Annexation by and Between Monticello Township
and the City of Monticello assigns the land use authority to the City of Monticello when a
petition for annexation has been received by the City; and
WHEREAS, the Applicant has also requested approval of zoning amendments related to the
establishment of the Edmonson Business Center Data Center Planned Unit Development district
(EBD-DCPUD) and rezoning other portions of the Property to Agricultural Open Space (A-O) and
Industrial Business Campus (IBC); and
WHEREAS, the Applicant has further requested approval of a Preliminary Plat, subdividing the
Property into lots and blocks, as well as outlots, in support of the land use applications; and
WHEREAS, the City has adopted language related to its policies for consideration of data center
development proposals in the Monticello 2040 Vision + Plan (Comprehensive Plan); and
WHEREAS, the City has further adopted Zoning Ordinance regulations intended to implement
the Comprehensive Plan policies for data centers, known as the Data Center Planned Unit
Development District (“DCPUD”); and
WHEREAS, the DCPUD provides for terms and requirements for approval, as well as specific
performance standards required for data center development and operation; and
WHEREAS, the Property is guided for Light Industrial Park land uses, as required by the City’s
land use regulations governing the consideration of data center applications; and
1

WHEREAS, the Conditional Use Permit is subject to the procedural and performance standards
of the DCPUD as found below; and
WHEREAS, the Planning Commission held a public hearing on October 6, 2026 on the
application and the applicant and members of the public were provided the opportunity to
present information to the Planning Commission; and
WHEREAS, the Planning Commission has considered all of the research, public comments, the
staff report, and conditions of approval, which are incorporated by reference into the
resolution; and
WHEREAS, the Planning Commission of the City of Monticello makes the following Findings of
Fact in relation to the recommendation of approval:
1. The City has researched the elements and impacts of data centers on the communities
and the areas surrounding such facilities.
2. This research has included reviews of published materials, personal visits to existing sites
and those under construction, and interviews with public officials where such facilities
have been constructed.
3. The City finds that past versions of data center development have often failed to provide
adequate consideration for neighboring land uses, community impacts on infrastructure,
and environmental concerns.
4. The City also finds that current versions of data center development have evolved to
incorporate more effective means to address issues raised by other examples.
5. The City finds that while data centers have had certain specific deleterious effects on
their surroundings, these effects are identifiable and regulations can be adopted that
address those effects.
6. The City finds that data centers have the potential to create significant positive impacts
for the communities in which they are located, including reliable tax base, provision of
public infrastructure expansions otherwise beyond local financial means, and other
impacts depending on their situation and locations.
7. The City finds that while data centers tend to create fewer numbers of permanent jobs
than comparable light industrial development, those jobs are often technical and well
paid.
8. The City finds that in addition to those permanent jobs, data center development
creates a large demand for construction jobs that, while “temporary”, can nonetheless
last for several years due to the phasing and scale of data center projects generating
positive local economic impacts.
2

9. After study, the City has adopted Comprehensive Plan policies that accommodate data
center development under specific conditions, designed to confirm that data center
projects provide the identified benefits, but adequately mitigate the potential negative
impacts of construction and/or operation.
10. The City has further adopted a specific zoning ordinance (the Data Center Planned Unit
Development District, Ordinance 865) to address both the impacts of and regulatory
standards for data center projects, as well as the many details learned from other
previous examples.
11. Under these regulations, the City has been provided with an application for a data
center campus, known as the Edmonson Business Center, on a collection of parcels of
approximately 547 acres in total.
12. The City has conducted an Alternative Urban Areawide Review environmental study of
the potential environmental impacts of the Edmonson Business Center concept, the
results and mitigation measures of which are incorporated into the City’s review and
approval of the Edmonson Business Center.
13. The City finds that the Edmonson Business Center will meet the City’s land use
requirements, including the following aspects:
a. The submitted application provides plans and documents found to be sufficient
with respect to the requirements of the DCPUD ordinance for Conditional Use
Permit, subject to Exhibit Z - Conditions of Approval.
b. The Edmonson Business Center will provide significant fiscal benefits to the City
of Monticello, benefitting its taxpayers and diversifying and enhancing the
community’s tax base.
c. The Edmonson Business Center will provide significant public infrastructure
expansions at the applicant’s cost, benefiting existing taxpayers, and facilitating
future growth that would otherwise be less likely without the project.
d. The Edmonson Business Center, in meeting the requirements of the City’s
comprehensive plan and land use regulations, will operate in a manner that
results in minimal negative impacts to the residential lands near the subject
property, including mitigation of potential noise, lights, traffic, water supply, and
other potential impacts.
e. These potential impacts are mitigated through the City’s requirement for
enhanced setback and buffering requirements for the Property, specific
standards for noise abatement and monitoring, the limitation on the projects use

3

of backup power generation capacity to 25 megawatts, and other applications of
the Data Center PUD Ordinance.
f. The Edmonson Business Center, based on the limitations of public water usage of
75,000 gallons per day based on an annual average and public wastewater
treatment of 75,000 gallons per day based on an annual average, will create
similar or lower demand on these systems than comparable industrial projects
that would otherwise be eligible land uses on the subject property.
g. The Edmonson Business Center will create only nominal impacts on the City’s
public services, including City water, sanitary sewer and wastewater treatment,
streets, parks and trails, or other services.
h. The Edmonson Business Center will provide the necessary multi-modal
transportation improvements to serve the site and to the benefit of the
community’s long-term growth.
i.

The DCPUD district ordinance establishes specific provisions to mitigate the
impacts of construction on the neighboring lands, including the restrictions on
construction hours, installation of berming and landscape screening before other
construction activities, along with other related regulations.

j.

The Edmonson Business Center will avoid negative impacts to wetlands by
excluding the existing wetlands on the site from the developable areas, with any
required wetland management or mitigation applicable.

k. The Edmonson Business Center will benefit the City’s open space and trail system
through the completion of trails along the perimeter roadways, as well as
through the construction of a trail across the south portion of the site near the
public water basin No. 394-W.
l.

The Edmonson Business Center will reserve the area along the public water basin
within the Shoreland Overlay District, protecting it from incompatible land uses
consistent with the Shoreland Overlay zoning restrictions.

14. With these findings, and subject to the recommendations of the staff report “Exhibit Z”
conditions, the City finds that the Edmonson Business Center will be consistent with the
requirements and direction of the City’s Comprehensive Plan (Monticello 2040 Vision +
Plan), the City’s Zoning and Subdivision Regulations, and all other relevant City Code
provisions.
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED, by the Planning Commission of the City of Monticello,
Minnesota, that the Planning Commission recommends to the City Council that the Conditional
Use Permit for data center and data center campus uses in the Edmonson Business Center Data
4

Center Planned Unit Development as provided in this resolution be approved, based on the
findings noted herein, and subject to the conditions identified in Exhibit Z of the Staff report, as
listed below:
1.

The applications for preliminary plat for Edmonson Business Center, zoning amendment
rezoning Lots 1 and 2, Block 1 to EDC-DCPUD, Conditional Use Permit for Data Center
and Data Center Campus in a DCPUD District, and rezonings to A-O and IBC are subject
to adoption of a resolution for annexation of the subject site and approval of the Final
Plat by the City Council, including execution and fulfillment of relevant agreements
governing installation of infrastructure and other provisions.

2.

No City permit for grading, construction, building or other development for the subject
site shall be approved unless the Preliminary and Final Plat, Zoning Amendments
(Rezoning), and Conditional Use Permit have been approved and all conditions have
been met to the fullest extent at the time of permitting.

3.

Site Plan Review submission and approval shall be required for any phase of grading,
construction, building or other development for the subject site. Submission shall be
per City Ordinance §153.028 for Site Plan Review, as it may be amended.

4.

No other uses of the EBC-DCPUD property, other than those expressly identified under
the terms of the EBC-DCPUD zoning district and the approvals granted to this
application, shall be permitted at any time over the life of the project.

5.

No activity or development on the property of any building, phase or full project may
result in a need for further environmental review subject to MN Rule 4410.4300 or
4410.4400 for subparts which were not reviewed by the Monticello Industrial AUAR.
Future end user site plans must not include construction or operational elements that
exceed mandatory environmental review thresholds which are not covered by the
AUAR. Any such violation shall be subject to any of the City’s legal remedies.

6.

Site thresholds for public water and sanitary sewer utilities for the EBC-DCPUD are
limited as follows and shall be subject to any applicable user agreement required by the
City no later than the time of connection to municipal system.
a. Average annual water demand shall not exceed 75,000 gallons per day; with
exception of irrigation for landscaping establishment and water recharge for closedloop cooling system.
b. Average annual wastewater discharge shall not exceed 75,000 gallons per day.
c. A schedule for recharging of the closed-loop cooling system shall be provided with
the applicable Site Plan and is subject to any required user agreement applicable at

5

the time of submission. In no case shall the closed-loop system demand exceed any
environmental review or water appropriations threshold.
d. The closed loop water resulting from recharge shall not be discharged to the
Monticello municipal system and a plan for the disposal of the cooling system water
shall be provided prior to any permit for the cooling system or connection to the
municipal system.
7.

Diesel backup power generation shall be limited to no more than 25 megawatts, shall
utilize generators which meet the lowest emission threshold, and in no case shall backup power generation of any type be of a generation capacity that necessitates further
state environmental review.

8.

All backup power sources shall meet the relevant performance standards of the DCPUD.

9.

The plans submitted as a part of this application shall be revised per the approved
conditions and at the time such plans are found to meet the required conditions, shall
become an integral part of the City’s approval of the EBC-DCPUD ordinance, Plat, and
Conditional Use Permit.

10.

All elements of the EBC-DCPUD, including those elements to be presented with Site Plan
review, shall comply with the Minimum Performance Standards of Ordinance 865DCPUD, including but not limited to building height, building materials and exterior
finishes, rooftop screening, parking, loading, and any other provisions of the DCPUD
ordinance.

11.

The power substation proposed in the DCPUD shall be subject to final location and size
required by Xcel Energy and any requirements of the PUC process or permit. All
applicable plans and documents for the applications shall be revised to detail the final
location of the proposed electrical substation and shall illustrate the substation as
located behind the required 10’ DCPUD berm.

12.

The applicant shall provide an amended site narrative and other related plans that
identify the dimensions of the DCPUD FAR area consistent with the final “Developable
Area” following resolution of the substation parcel size.

13.

The “Developable Area” as shown in the Site Improvements Plan shall constitute the
applicable setback and limits of construction for principal Data Center and Data Center
Campus improvements, except those accessory uses identified in Condition 15 below.
Any expansion of the Developable Area or development of principal uses outside the
Developable Area shall require an amendment to the EBC-PUD and Conditional Use
Permit.

6

14.

The Developable Area Plan sheet of the Site Improvements Plan shall be revised as
follows:
a. The “Developable Area” as depicted shall be revised to illustrate the area’s
dimensions.
b. Dimension the applicable setbacks based for the “Developable Area” as shown.
c. Remove notation for “schematic” and “subject to change”.
d. Remove the “Permitting Required if Impacted' notation from the two wetlands east
of the proposed Development Area.
e. Stormwater locations specifically may include a notation for “subject to change
based on required stormwater permitting and review”.
f. Provide a detailed large-scale sheet for the northwest portion of the site detailing
setbacks for principal uses per the required “Developable Area” Plan and accessory
use setbacks per Ordinance 865-DCPUD.
g. Remove notation for “Proposed” for the Developable Area within Lot 1,
Block 1.

15.

Accessory uses on the property as listed below shall meet the required Accessory Use
Setbacks per Ordinance 865 – DCPUD and shall comply with the following minimum
standards:
a. Parking lots and access drives shall comply with the City’s off-street parking
regulations, Section §153.067.
b. Security fencing shall be located inside the berm as on the Site Improvement Plans
and shall comply with Ordinance 865 - DCPUD.
c. Lighting shall comply with the requirements of the City’s lighting regulations, Section
§153.063 except as further required by Ordinance 865 - DCPUD.
d. Berm and landscaping shall be per plans as required to be revised by these
conditions and shall be subject to Section §153.060 except as further required by
Ordinance 865-DCPUD.
e. Security building(s) shall be subject to Ordinance 865 – DCPUD for accessory
buildings.
f. Office buildings which are not principal use data centers shall be subject to
Ordinance 865-DCPUD for accessory buildings.

16.

Due to the potential for noise, visual or other impacts, and given their nature in
supporting the principal use, all other appurtenant or accessory uses and activity that is
7

not temporary construction activity, which shall include but not be limited to, off-street
loading associated with the principal use, backup power generation, mechanical
equipment, fuel storage, or any other such similar activity must be located within the
“Developable Area” as depicted and be screened per the DCPUD ordinance and Zoning
Ordinance Section 153.060 (I)(5), Rooftop Screening.
17.

Temporary activities shall be subject to the requirements of Ordinance 865- DCPUD, and
the following clarifying conditions:
a. The applicant shall provide a construction phasing plan acceptable to the City
Engineer for Site Plan Review prior to the initiation of any site work, and work in the
DCPUD shall be limited to the relevant permitted phase or phases.
b. Construction staging, activities, materials and equipment must be located within the
bermed area of the site and be fully screened from directly adjacent parcel ground
level views, excepting construction equipment that exceeds the height of the
landscaped berm, and construction activities and equipment associated with initial
grading and berm construction.
c. Any off-site construction staging shall require separate approvals from applicable
agencies, and any City comments on conditions to accommodate construction traffic
and staging from off-site locations.
d. Temporary construction trailers, sanitation facilities, and storage containers may be
used for storage and site construction purposes within the Developable Area of the
site.
e. Buildings utilized for office spaces shall meet the minimum building materials
standards, and/or construction offices and other related uses (including storage)
may be housed in buildings constructed as temporary post-frame metal structures.
f. All parking and storage shall be located on paved surfaces.
g. The City must approve any developer-proposed construction haul-routes to and
from the site.
h. The applicant must provide a deposit for dust control on the site during
construction.
i.

A site sanitation plan shall be provided for each phase of construction.

j.

The applicant may install a hydrant for water service on the site through use of a
City-approved temporary meter.

k. Construction hours shall be as listed in the DCPUD zoning district, which are 8:00am
to 7:00pm, Monday through Saturday.
8

l.

All temporary structures and uses shall be removed from the site within 30 days of
final Certificates of Occupancy for any phase.

m. Temporary uses permitted as a part of the project construction shall be extinguished
when no longer necessary to conduct the relevant work on the site, including
removal of any temporary buildings and any other improvements, storage,
materials, or other elements. Removal of such improvements shall require
immediate restoration of the site subject to approval of any phase of development
in that location and in accordance with the approved phasing plan.
n. No construction activity shall be allowed in any portion of the project, temporary or
otherwise, for which a Site Plan with phasing has not been submitted and approved.
18.

No wetland impacts are permitted within the plat and EBC-DCPUD area, excepting
submission of a wetland sequencing application prior to final plat, or such modification
required as part of a PUC permitting process.

19.

The applicant must supply a wetland vegetation management plan for wetlands within
the DCPUD area.

20.

The applicant shall prepare at their sole cost the Intersection Control Evaluation
required by MnDOT in their comment letter of September 29, 2026 and shall provide for
the implementation of the infrastructure recommendations therein at their sole cost.

21.

The applicant shall prepare a revised Ambient Noise Study which complies with MPCA
Guidelines for a one-week period prior to construction of the berm.

22.

Subsequent sound studies shall adhere to Ordinance 865-DCPUD and with the following
additional requirements:
a. An independent, third-party engineer shall prepare the required sound studies and
resulting mitigation plans demonstrating compliance with MPCA Rules, which shall
be subject to the review and approval of the City. The developer shall provide a
security for such review.
b. Operational noise study shall be provided with the first phase building construction.
c. Once operational, sound studies per Ordinance 865-DCPUD shall be completed in
July of each year and with each phase of principal building construction.
d. A Noise Impact Study per City Ordinance §130.09 shall be prepared for lowfrequency sound by an independent, third-party engineer. Such study shall be
accompanied by a subsequent mitigation plan, which shall be subject to the review
of the City’s designated sound engineer consultant and approval of the City. The
developer shall provide a security for such review.
9

23.

The applicant shall designate a liaison for reports of noise violations and complaints
emanating from the DCPUD with the first Site Plan Approval and update such contact
information throughout the life of the project with the City’s Department of Building
Safety & Code Enforcement. Such liaison shall be responsible for evaluating response
and communicating information on any required mitigation.

24.

Right of Way dedication for the plat shall be required as follows:
a. Right of way for Davidson Avenue NE to be dedicated to full width per City
Engineer’s report, and constructed to urban standard to the limits of the plat.
b. Right of way for 85th Street NE to be dedicated for the full width (as needed) per the
City Engineer’s report, and constructed to urban standard to the limits of the plat.
c. Right of way for Edmonson Avenue NE to be dedicated for the full width and
constructed to a rural standard per the requirements of Wright County and the City
Engineer’s report.
d. If an outlot is not provided, right of way for utilities shall be provided between
Outlots C and D at a width of 150’.
e. Right of way as required by the City Engineer to serve required or future
roundabouts at the intersection of 85th Street NE and the intersection of TH 25 and
85th Street NE and Edmonson Avenue.

25.

The plat shall be updated to reflect the required 6’ lot and 12’ plat perimeter easements
per the Subdivision Ordinance.

26.

Berm Requirements:
a. The applicant shall provide for construction of the berm and related screening
plantings as a first phase of work on the site, prior to any other development work.
Screening requirements of the DCPUD shall be waived for grading and construction
of the berm. The City may approve other development work, at its sole discretion,
once screening has been established and would be effective in portions of the EBCDCPUD.
b. Construction of the berm shall start in areas closest to adjoining residential uses.
c. Height of the berm must be adjusted to demonstrate compliance with the City’s
requirement that berm is no less than 10 feet above adjoining property lines, and/or
the adjoining roadway centerline, as applicable.
d. The applicant shall provide for temporary irrigation for landscape plantings on the
berm to enhance survival of the materials. Such irrigation shall be exempt from the
75,000 gallons per day water use otherwise applicable for the project.
10

e. The berm shall be placed to the inside of the required drainage and utility
easements on the site.
27.

Landscaping and screening for the DCPUD shall be revised to be consistent with the
following requirements:
a. Tree removals shall be limited to those shown on the Tree Removal Plan.
b. Revise landscaping plan to place conifers on the berm sides, particularly east and
southeast slopes, where soil depth and shelter improve survival. Plantings should be
shown in staggered, mixed-species rows (cedar, pine, spruce) diffuse wind force and
strengthen the screen. Shade trees are to be layered behind conifers in staggered
rows, offset to interlock canopies.
c. Increase canopy diversity with oaks, hackberry, hybrid elms, and other natives listed
in the City’s tree manual, while trimming back ornamental counts.
d. Increase canopy tree proportion to 30–35%, reduce ornamentals to 20–25%, and
maintain conifers at 30–35%.
e. Where power line corridors may interfere with planting plans, a customized
screening plan must be developed at the time of Site Plan review.
f. Exclude shrubs from DBH replacement schedule and clarify height-based listings
(such as conifers at 6 ft. B&B). Recalculate and revise landscaping plan to meet
replacement.
g. Revise plan to provide planting specification requiring lant materials to be sized to
provide a full screen within three years of planting.
h. Enhance native buffer plantings beyond minimum compliance. The applicant’s plans
should be amended to add hardwood seeding, prairie mixes, wildflower prairies, and
shrub thickets. These additions will strengthen natural views, improve biodiversity,
and provide long term resilience.
i.

Install a dedicated irrigation line for berm plantings to ensure proper establishment
and long term survival. This irrigation may be temporary until plant materials are
established and healthy.

j.

Show seeding specifications in all locations.

k. Additional landscaping within the Developable Area will be reviewed at the time of
Site Plan review.
28.

A warranty deed shall be provided conveying the proposed Lot 1, Block 2 to the City of
Monticello.

11

29.

In lieu of right of way, a warranty deed shall be provided conveying 150’ in width for the
public utility and pathway corridor between Outlots C and D.

30.

Accessory Battery Energy Storage Systems, if employed, shall require a separate
emergency services evaluation to be provided with the Site Plan review application
applicable to its installation and payment for any related preparedness shall be required
as determined by the City.

31.

Trash enclosures shall be located within buildings meeting the materials requirements
of the City’s zoning ordinance and the DCPUD and shall be located within the
Developable Area of the Site Improvements Plan.

32.

Site landscaping is completed as approved, with required maintenance securities as
specified in the Zoning Ordinance. Compliance with the approved landscaping plan is an
ongoing requirement of CUP and site plan approval. Materials that are removed or fail
to survive shall be replaced, regardless of landscape guarantees.

33.

The applicant shall execute and fulfill the terms of an annexation agreement and
development contract will provides for the applicant’s responsibility for all costs
associated with the development and operation of the project, including municipal
utilities, transportation, and any other public services or infrastructure necessary to
support the DCPUD. This includes infrastructure improvements or mitigation for the
expected public infrastructure impact or capacity increase created or required by the
DCPUD, and will identify a timeline satisfactory to the City for the submission of
payments and securities for such infrastructure.

34.

All public improvement plans are to be prepared at the applicant’s expense, whether
publicly or privately installed, and regardless of location.

35.

Provide opportunities for, and coordinate with, the City’s decision to provide Fibernet
installations in adjoining rights of way (installation at City cost and discretion).

36.

Existing irrigation wells on site are required to be capped in accordance with the
appropriate agency requirements

37.

The applicant and Xcel Energy shall execute a shared access agreement providing for
such access and maintenance of the shared access improvements to the electrical
substation.

38.

Any approvals granted under this application shall incorporate each of the required
mitigation measures of the AUAR Mitigation Plan adopted conducted for this project
area.

39.

All requirements of the Monticello City Code, where not otherwise specifically
addressed by this application or the DCPUD ordinance, shall be met.
12

40.

The applicant shall be required to update all plan submissions as related to Exhibit Z Conditions of Approval, Items 11, 12, 14, 19-20, 24-25, 26 b.-e., 27-29, and 33 prior to
consideration of the Final Plat.

41.

All plan submissions submitted for permitting or Site Plan Review shall be consistent
with the terms and conditions of the City Council approvals.

42.

Compliance with the terms of the City’s Engineering Staff letter dated September 28,
2026, and including the Mitigation Plan Comments of September 14, 2028.

43.

Comments and recommendations of other Staff and Planning Commission.

ADOPTED this 6th day of October, 2026 by the Planning Commission of the City of Monticello,
Minnesota.

CITY OF MONTICELLO
WRIGHT COUNTY, MINNESOTA
AN ORDINANCE AMENDING MONTICELLO ZONING ORDINANCE §153.047
FOR THE EDMONSON BUSINESS CENTER DATA CENTER
PLANNED UNIT DEVELOPMENT DISTRICT (EBC-DCPUD)
LOTS 1 AND 2, BLOCK 1, EDMONSON BUSINESS CENTER
THE CITY COUNCIL OF THE CITY OF MONTICELLO HEREBY ORDAINS:
Section 1.

Section §153.047 is hereby amended to add the following:
(AA)

Edmonson Business Center Data Center PUD District (EBC-DCPUD)
(1) Purpose. The purpose of the EBC-DCPUD District is to provide for the
development of certain real estate for data center and data center
campus uses subject to this District.
(2) No rezoning, plat, or conditional use permit shall be effective unless
and until the City has annexed the subject property, and the City and
applicant have executed an annexation agreement addressing the
terms of annexation.
(3) Permitted Uses. Permitted principal uses in the EBC-DCPUD shall be
data centers and data center campus as defined by City of Monticello
Ordinance 865 – Data Center Planned Unit Development (“DCPUD
Ordinance 865”), according to the approved Site Improvements Plans
and other submitted application materials provided on August 11,
2026, and as updated for approval conditions dated _____, 2026, the
development agreement dated ____, 2026, and the annexation
agreement dated _____, 2026, as may be amended. The introduction
of any other use from any other district shall be prohibited. All phases
of development shall require Site Plan Review approval per City
Ordinance City Ordinance §153.028, as it may be amended.
(4) DCPUD Flexibility. The EBC-DCPUD District provides flexibility from
the City’s standard zoning regulations for the following elements:
(a) Accommodates the construction of multiple principal buildings
within the Developable Area as shown on the approved Site
1

ORDINANCE NO. ___
Improvements Plan.
(b) Accommodates the accessory and appurtenant uses as allowed
for Data Center Campus as defined by DCPUD Ordinance 865.
(c) Accommodates the phased construction of the EBC-DCPUD as
shown on the approved Site Improvements Plan and subsequent
individual construction phasing plans.
(d) Accommodates the future design, layout, and construction of data
center buildings and accessory and appurtenant improvements
through City Site Plan Review process, subject to compliance with
all applicable regulations.
(e) Requires preparation and development of the required berm and
public infrastructure necessary to serve the site per the terms of
the approved Annexation Agreement as a precondition for any
construction within the Developable Area.
(5) Accessory Uses. Accessory uses shall be only those accessory uses
and buildings as allowed by the DCPUD District, Ordinance 865, and
as specifically provided for in the approved EBC-DCPUD District
documents.
(6) District Performance Standards. Performance standards for the
development of any building or other construction activity in the EBCDCPUD District shall adhere to the approved plans and development
agreement and the Minimum Performance Standards of DCPUD
Ordinance 865. The following requirements shall apply:
(a) Landscape planting within the applicable setbacks for the EBCDCPUD shall be reviewed by phase with the applicable Site Plan
Review.
(b) No activity or development on the property of any building, phase
or full project may result in a need for further environmental
review subject to MN Rule 4410.4300 or 4410.4400 for subparts
which were not reviewed by the Monticello Industrial AUAR.
Future end user site plans must not include construction or
operational elements that exceed mandatory environmental
review thresholds which are not covered by the AUAR. Any such
violation shall be subject to any of the City’s legal remedies.
(c) Site thresholds for public water and sanitary sewer utilities are
limited as follows.
(i) Average annual water demand shall not exceed 75,000
gallons per day; with exception of irrigation for
2

ORDINANCE NO. ___
landscaping establishment and water recharge for closedloop cooling system.
(ii) Average annual wastewater discharge shall not exceed
75,000 gallons per day to the municipal system.
(iii)A schedule for recharging of the closed-loop cooling
system shall be provided with the applicable Site Plan and
is subject to any required user agreement applicable no
later than the time of connection to municipal system. In
no case shall the closed-loop system demand exceed any
environmental review or water appropriations threshold.
(iv) The closed loop water resulting from recharge shall not be
discharged to the Monticello municipal system and a plan
for the disposal of the cooling system water shall be
provided prior to any permit for the cooling system or
connection to the municipal system.
(d) Setbacks for principal use data center buildings in the EBC-DCPUD
shall be defined by the “Developable Area” shown on the
approved Site Improvements Plan. All principal data center
buildings and their appurtenant uses shall be located within the
Developable Area.
(e) Due to the potential for noise, visual, or other impacts, and given
their nature in supporting the principal use, setbacks for all
appurtenant or supporting activity such as permanent mechanical
equipment and yards, off-street loading for data center buildings,
back-up power generation, fuel storage, and other similar uses
shall be within the Developable Area and screened per the DCPUD
ordinance and Zoning Ordinance Section 153.060 (I)(5), Rooftop
Screening.
(f) Setbacks for the electric substation shall be compliant with
DCPUD Ordinance 865 requirements. The substation shall be
located behind the required 10’ DCPUD berm.
(g) The following accessory uses on the property may be located
outside of the Developable Area, but shall be located within the
minimum applicable setback and comply with the following
minimum standards:
(i) Parking lots and access drives shall be subject to the City’s
off-street parking regulations, Section §153.067.
(ii) Fencing shall be subject to the fencing and walls
requirements of the DCPUD Ordinance 865.
(iii)Lighting shall be subject to the lighting requirements of
the DCPUD Ordinance 865.
3

ORDINANCE NO. ___
(iv) Berm and landscaping shall be subject to the requirements
of DCPUD Ordinance 865, which includes a requirement
for a berm with a minimum height of 10’ above the
adjoining property line or public right of way
encompassing the DCPUD.
(v) Security building(s) shall be subject to the requirements of
the DCPUD Ordinance 865.
(vi) Accessory office buildings which are not principal use datacenter buildings shall be subject to the standards of the
DCPUD Ordinance 865 for accessory buildings.
(h) No outdoor storage of equipment or materials shall be allowed in
the EBC-DCPUD, except that as directly allowed and regulated for
temporary construction uses per this ordinance.
(i) No wetland impacts are permitted within the plat and EBC-DCPUD
area, excepting submission of a wetland sequencing application
prior to final plat, or such modification required as part of a PUC
permitting process.
(j) Diesel backup power generation shall be limited to no more than
25 megawatts, shall utilize generators which meet the lowest
emission threshold, and in no case shall back-up power
generation of any type be of a generation capacity that
necessitates further state environmental review.
(k) All backup power sources shall meet the relevant performance
standards of the DCPUD.
(l) Sound studies shall adhere to Ordinance 865-DCPUD and with the
following additional requirements:
(i) An independent, third-party engineer shall prepare the
required sound studies and resulting mitigation plans
demonstrating compliance with MPCA Rules, which shall
be subject to the review and approval of the City.
(ii) Operational noise study shall be provided with the first
phase building construction.
(iii)Once operational, sound studies per Ordinance 865DCPUD shall be completed in July of each year and with
each phase of principal building construction.
(iv) A Noise Impact Study per City Ordinance §130.09 shall be
prepared for low-frequency sound by an independent,
third-party engineer. Such study shall be accompanied by
a subsequent mitigation plan, which shall be subject to the
4

ORDINANCE NO. ___
review of the City’s designated sound engineer consultant
and approval of the City.
(v) A liaison for reports of noise violations and complaints
emanating from the DCPUD with the first Site Plan
Approval and update such contact information throughout
the life of the project with the City’s Department of
Building Safety & Code Enforcement. Such liaison shall be
responsible for evaluating response and communicating
information on any required mitigation.
(m) Construction yard and staging. Unless otherwise properly
permitted by the appropriate agency, the construction yard shall
be located within the berm as approved for the EBC-DCPUD and
subject to all requirements of the district, including the following:
(i)

A construction phasing plan acceptable to the City
Engineer shall be submitted for Site Plan Review and is
required prior to the initiation of any site work, and work
in the DCPUD shall be limited to the relevant permitted
phase or phases.

(ii)

Construction staging, activities, materials and equipment
must be located within the bermed area of the site, and
be fully screened from ground level views, excepting
construction equipment that exceeds the height of the
landscaped berm, and construction activities and
equipment associated with initial grading and berm
construction.

(iii) Any off-site construction staging shall require separate

comments on conditions to accommodate construction
traffic and staging from off-site locations.
(iv) Temporary construction trailers, sanitation facilities, and
storage containers may be used for storage and site
construction purposes within the Developable Area of
the site.
(v)

Buildings utilized for office spaces shall meet the

construction offices and other related uses (including
storage) may be housed in buildings constructed as
temporary post-frame metal structures.

(vi) All parking and storage shall be located on paved
surfaces.

ORDINANCE NO. ___
(vii) The City must approve any developer-proposed
construction haul-routes to and from the site.
(viii) The applicant must provide a deposit for dust control on
the site during construction.
(ix) A site sanitation plan shall be provided for each phase of
construction.
(x)

The applicant may install a hydrant for water service on
the site through use of a City-approved temporary
meter.

(xi) Construction hours shall be as listed in the Ordinance
865 -DCPUD, which are 8:00am to 7:00pm, Monday
through Saturday.
(xii) All temporary structures and uses shall be removed from
the site within 30 days of final Certificates of Occupancy
for any phase.
(xiii) Temporary uses permitted as a part of the project
construction shall be extinguished when no longer
necessary to conduct the relevant work on the site,
including removal of any temporary buildings and any
other improvements, storage, materials, or other
elements. Removal of such improvements shall require
immediate restoration of the site subject to approval of
any phase of development in that location and in
accordance with the approved phasing plan.
(xiv) No construction activity shall be allowed in any portion
of the project, temporary or otherwise, for which a Site
Plan with phasing has not been submitted and approved.
(n) Site landscaping is completed as approved, with required
maintenance securities as specified in the Zoning Ordinance.
Compliance with the approved landscaping plan is an ongoing

(o) All other requirements of the Monticello Zoning Ordinance shall
apply except as otherwise stated.
(p) All conditions of approval for the EBC-DCPUD as adopted by City
Council resolutions approving the Zoning Amendments,
Preliminary and Final Plat, and Conditional Use Permit are
incorporated into these Performance Standards.
6

ORDINANCE NO. ___
(7) Final approval of this ordinance is only effective upon satisfaction of
all conditions and compliance with Section (2) of this ordinance,
which shall be prior to annexation and plat recording, except those
that are ongoing conditions of operational functions, or designated
conditions by phase as identified.
(8) The plans and other documents submitted with the application for
the EBC-DCPUD, as amended and updated per City requirements, are
incorporated into the requirements of the EBC-DCPUD.
(9) Amendments. Where changes to the EBC-DCPUD are proposed in the
manner of use, density, Developable Area, building square footage,
mass, or coverage, or any other change, the proposer shall be
required to apply for an amendment to the PUD under the terms of
City Ordinance No. 865 regulating data center development in the
city. The City may require that substantial changes in overall use of
the EBC-DCPUD property be processed as a new application, including
a zoning district amendment.
Section 2.

The City Clerk is hereby directed to mark the official zoning map to reflect this
ordinance. The map shall not be republished at this time.

Section 3.

The City Clerk is hereby directed to make the changes required by this Ordinance
as part of the Official Monticello City Code, Title 10, Zoning Ordinance, and to
renumber the tables and chapters accordingly as necessary to provide the
intended effect of this Ordinance. The City Clerk is further directed to make
necessary corrections to any internal citations that result from said renumbering
process, provided that such changes retain the purpose and intent of the Zoning
Ordinance as has been adopted.

Section 4.

This Ordinance shall take effect and be in full force from and after its passage
and publication. The ordinance in its entirety and map shall be posted on the
City website after publication. Copies of the complete Ordinance and map are
available online and at Monticello City Hall for examination upon request.

CITY OF MONTICELLO
WRIGHT COUNTY, MINNESOTA
AN ORDINANCE AMENDING TITLE XV OF THE MONTICELLO CITY CODE, KNOWN AS THE
ZONING ORDINANCE, AMENDING THE MONTICELLO OFFICIAL ZONING MAP BY REZONING
THE FOLLOWING PROPERTY IBC INDUSTRIAL BUSINESS CAMPUS DISTRICT AND A-O,
AGRICULTURE-OPEN SPACE DISTRICT
THE CITY COUNCIL OF THE CITY OF MONTICELLO HEREBY ORDAINS:
Section 1.

The Official Zoning Map of the City of Monticello is hereby amended by rezoning
the proposed Lot 1, Block 2 and Outlots A and B, Edmonson Business Center to
IBC, Industrial Business Campus District.

Section 2.

The Official Zoning Map of the City of Monticello is further hereby amended by
rezoning the proposed Outlots C and D, including the right of way area between
Outlots C and D, Edmonson Business Center to A-O, Agriculture-Open Space
District.

Section 3.

The City Clerk is hereby directed to mark the official zoning map to reflect this
ordinance. The map shall not be republished at this time.

Section 4.

The City Clerk is hereby directed to make the changes required by this Ordinance
as part of the Official Monticello City Code, Title 10, Zoning Ordinance, and to
renumber the tables and chapters accordingly as necessary to provide the
intended effect of this Ordinance. The City Clerk is further directed to make
necessary corrections to any internal citations that result from said renumbering
process, provided that such changes retain the purpose and intent of the Zoning
Ordinance as has been adopted.

Section 5.

This Ordinance shall take effect and be in full force from and after its passage
and publication. The ordinance in its entirety and map shall be posted on the
City website after publication. Copies of the complete Ordinance and map are
available online and at Monticello City Hall for examination upon request.

ADOPTED BY the Monticello City Council this ____ day of ______, 2026.

Power Transmission and Distribution
The exact electrical demand of the proposed data center campus will be determined during
subsequent design phases based on the requirements of the future end user. Current planning
assumes a campus electrical demand in excess of 500 MW at full buildout. To evaluate the feasibility
of serving this demand, Xcel Energy completed a System Impact Study (SIS) that analyzed the
anticipated power requirements and interconnection to the regional transmission system. The study
concluded that the project can be adequately served, subject to the completion of identified
transmission system upgrades, all of which would be funded by the project developer or end
user/operator. Service will be provided via interconnection to Xcel Energy’s 345kV transmission
system in the Monticello area.

Service Approach and Conceptual Transmission Corridors
Service will be provided via interconnection to Xcel Energy’s 345kV transmission system in the
Monticello area.
The conceptual service approach includes:
 A new substation to serve the project site
 Interconnection to existing Elm Creek–Monticello and Elm Creek–Big Oaks 345kV transmission
lines

ATTACHMENT A - PUBLIC BENEFIT ANALYSIS

PUBLIC BENEFIT ANALYSIS

Public Infrastructure and Property Tax Investment
The proposed development delivers substantial, quantifiable public benefit in two distinct forms: a privatelyfunded extension of regional sewer and water infrastructure, and a significant increase in the local property
tax base. Together, these benefits represent a direct investment in the City's long-term capital position and
fiscal capacity, achieved at no cost to existing ratepayers or taxpayers.

Regional Sewer and Water Infrastructure Extension
The proposed development includes a privately-funded sewer and water utility extension representing an
investment in excess of $70,000,000. This infrastructure will be designed with capacity beyond the immediate
needs of the subject property, extending service availability to adjacent and nearby parcels that are currently
unserved by municipal utilities.
• No cost to the City or existing ratepayers. The full cost of design, engineering, and construction will

be borne solely by the developer, with no reliance on public bonds, tax increment financing, or
municipal capital improvement funds. This represents a direct cost avoidance for the City, which would
otherwise need to program this extension through its own capital improvement plan — a project of this
scale that could otherwise take years, if not decades, to fund through traditional municipal budgeting.
• Catalyst for surrounding area development. By extending trunk-line capacity beyond the boundaries

of the subject property, this investment removes a key infrastructure barrier that has historically
constrained development on adjacent and nearby parcels, creating the potential for additional private
investment, expanded tax base, and future development in the surrounding area without further public
expenditure on utility extension.
• Long-term operational and financial savings. Because the infrastructure will be constructed to

municipal standards and dedicated to the City upon completion, the City will gain ownership of a
significant capital asset without having incurred the upfront cost — avoiding future costs to
independently finance, design, and construct comparable infrastructure, while gaining new connection
and utility revenue from both the subject property and any subsequent development it enables.
• Right-sized regional investment. Rather than sizing the extension only to meet the needs of the subject

development, the developer is investing in infrastructure sized to serve a broader service area —
effectively subsidizing regional growth capacity that benefits the community at large.

Property Tax Base Increase
In addition to the infrastructure investment described above, the proposed development is projected to
generate a substantial and durable increase in the local property tax base. Preliminary annual property tax
projections compare the current taxable value and tax generation of the existing parcels with the projected
valuation under the proposed development.

For planning purposes, the analysis assumes approximately 2.0 million square feet of development at an
estimated taxable value of $150 per square foot, resulting in a projected taxable value of approximately $300
million. Annual property tax revenue was estimated using current tax rates applicable to the Northern States
Power Company power plant. For purposes of the tax revenue projections only, the analysis assumes
construction beginning in Quarter 1 of 2028 and reaching completion in Quarter 4 of 2030.

The existing parcels have a combined taxable value of approximately $2,909,300 and generate approximately
$17,178 in annual property taxes under current conditions. Under the projected data center development,
taxable value is estimated to increase to approximately $300,000,000, generating projected annual property
tax revenues of approximately $7,001,817. On this basis, the proposed development is estimated to increase
taxable value by approximately $297,090,700 and to increase annual property tax revenue by approximately
$6,984,639 relative to the existing condition — a recurring, compounding source of revenue for the City and
other overlapping taxing jurisdictions, generated without additional demand on municipal services typical of
comparable-value residential development.

Summary
Taken together, the sewer and water infrastructure extension and the projected increase in property tax
revenue represent complementary, mutually reinforcing public benefits. The infrastructure investment
expands regional service capacity and removes a longstanding barrier to development on surrounding parcels,
while the property tax increase provides the City and overlapping taxing jurisdictions with a substantial,
recurring new revenue stream. Both benefits are achieved through private investment, at no direct cost to the
City or its existing ratepayers and taxpayers.
Note: Figures presented are preliminary estimates prepared for planning purposes and are subject to revision based on final assessed
valuation, applicable millage rates, and engineering design of the utility extension.

MPCA DUTY OFFICER 24 HOUR EMERGENCY NOTIFCATION: 651-649-5451 OR 800-422-0798
GENERAL SWPPP NOTES FOR CONSTRUCTION ACTIVITY:
1. THE GENERAL CONTRACTOR IS RESPONSIBLE TO COMPLY WITH ALL ASPECTS OF THE NPDES CONSTRUCTION STORMWATER PERMIT AT ALL TIMES UNTIL THE NOTICE
OF TERMINATION (NOT) HAS BEEN FILED WITH THE MPCA. THE CONTRACTOR WILL DEVELOP A CHAIN OF COMMAND WITH ALL OPERATORS ON THE SITE TO ENSURE
THAT THE SWPPP WILL BE IMPLEMENTED AND STAY IN EFFECT UNTIL THE CONSTRUCTION PROJECT IS COMPLETE, THE ENTIRE SITE HAS UNDERGONE FINAL
STABILIZATION, AND A NOTICE OF TERMINATION (NOT) HAS BEEN SUBMITTED TO THE MPCA.
2.

THE CONTRACTOR WILL PREPARE A WRITTEN, NOT ORAL, WEEKLY SCHEDULE OF PROPOSED EROSION CONTROL ACTIVITIES FOR THE PROJECT ENGINEERS APPROVAL
AS PER MN/DOT SPEC. 1717.

3.

THE CONTRACTOR WILL PREPARE AND SUBMIT A SITE PLAN FOR THE ENGINEERS APPROVAL AS PER MN/DOT SPEC. 1717 FOR CONCRETE MANAGEMENT, WORK IN
ENVIRONMENTALLY SENSITIVE AREAS, AREAS IDENTIFIED IN THE PLANS AS “SITE PLAN REQUIREMENT AREA.” ANY WORK THAT WILL REQUIRE DEWATERING, THE
STAGING OF INLET PROTECTION DEVICES OVER THE LIFE OF THE CONTRACT AND AS REQUESTED BY THE ENGINEER, ALL SITE PLANS MUST BE SUBMITTED TO THE
ENGINEER IN WRITING. THE CONTRACTOR SHALL ALLOW A MINIMUM OF 7 DAYS FOR REVIEW AND APPROVE SITE PLAN SUBMITTALS. THE CONTRACTOR WILL NOT BE
ALLOWED TO COMMENCE WORK FOR WHICH A SITE PLAN IS REQUIRED UNTIL APPROVAL HAS BEEN GRANTED BY THE ENGINEER. THE CONTRACTOR WILL NOT BE
GIVEN ANY EXTRA TIME IN THE CONTRACT DUE TO THE UNTIMELY SUBMITTAL OF A SITE PLAN. THE CONTRACTOR SHALL SUBMIT A CONTRACTOR'S EROSION /
SEDIMENT CONTROL SITE PLAN TO THE ENGINEER A MINIMUM OF 24 HOURS PRIOR TO THE FIRST PRE-CONSTRUCTION MEETING. THE CONTRACTOR'S EROSION /
SEDIMENT CONTROL SITE PLAN SHALL INCLUDE:
A. THE NAME OF THE CONTRACTOR'S DESIGNATED EROSION CONTROL SUPERVISOR WITH 24-HOUR CONTACT INFORMATION. (I.E. PHONE NUMBER, EMAIL, ETC.)
B. NAME AND CONTACT INFORMATION FOR THE INDIVIDUAL(S) RESPONSIBLE FOR PERFORMINGAND MAINTAINING THE SITE INSPECTION LOGS ON A WEEKLY BASIS
(OR WITHIN 24 HOURS OF 0.5 INCHES OF A RAIN IN A 24 HOUR PERIOD).
C. NAME AND CONTACT INFORMATION OF THE INDIVIDUAL(S) RESPONSIBLE FOR TEMPORARY AND PERMANENT STABILIZATION.
D. NAME AND CONTACT INFORMATION OF THE INDIVIDUAL(S) WHO WILL BE RESPONSIBLE FOR EMERGENCY REPAIRS AND REPLACEMENTS.
E. LOCATION WHERE THE SWPPP DOCUMENT AND NPDES PERMIT WILL BE KEPT ON-SITE. THE DOCUMENT SHOULD BE ACCESSIBLE AT ALL TIMES AND AVAILABLE
IN THE TIME OF ESSENCE.
F. WHERE AND HOW CONCRETE WASHOUT WILL OCCUR AND BE IN COMPLIANCE OF THE MINNESOTA POLLUTION CONTROL AGENCY'S (MPCA) MEMORANDUM
“CONCRETE WASHOUT GUIDANCE”.
G. LOCATION OF STOCKPILES OF NATIVE SOILS AND/OR BORROW MATERIALS AND INDICATE HOW STOCKPILES WILL BE KEPT IN COMPLIANCE WITH NPDES AND
MPCA REQUIREMENTS.
H. LOCATION OF ANY TEMPORARY SEDIMENT BASINS AND TRAPS.

4.

THE CONTRACTOR WILL COMPLY WITH THE REQUIREMENTS REGARDING POLLUTION PREVENTION MANAGEMENT DURING CONSTRUCTION, WHICH WILL INCLUDE, BUT
NOT LIMITED TO PROVIDING:
A. CONCRETE WASHOUT AREAS FOR USE BY ALL SUBCONTRACTORS AND PROJECT PERSONNEL. LOCATION OF WASHOUT AREAS MUST BE IDENTIFIED BY
SIGNAGE AND MUST BE AT LEAST 200' FROM SITE PLAN REQUIREMENT AREAS OR ENVIRONMENTALLY SENSITIVE AREAS, AND UTILIZE A LEAK-PROOF
CONTAINMENT FACILITY OR IMPERMEABLE LINER THAT PREVENTS RUNOFF ONTO ADJACENT SOILS. AN ENGINEERED COLLECTION SYSTEM CAN ALSO BE USED
IF IT IS APPROVED BY THE PROJECT ENGINEER.
B. SOLID WASTE COLLECTION AND REMOVAL
C. SECONDARY CONTAINMENT
D. SECURED HAZARDOUS WASTE STORAGE CONTAINERS
E. CHEMICAL SPILL KITS
F. PORTABLE RESTROOM FACILITIES THAT ARE ANCHORED TO PREVENT TIPPING

MODIFICATIONS TO EXISTING CONDITIONS/DRAINAGE PATTERNS:
THE EXISTING SITE IS SPLIT INTO FOUR DRAINAGE AREAS WITH OUTLETS TO LANDLOCKED BASINS AND EXISTING DRAINAGE DITCHES ON THE NORTH, EAST, AND WEST
BORDERS OF THE SITE. THESE DRAINAGE AREAS OUTFALL TO TWO LOCATIONS, DNR BASIN 86039400 AND A LAND LOCKED BASIN IN THE NORTH EAST CORNER. THE
PROPOSED DRAINAGE SYSTEM WILL ROUTE RUNOFF TO THE PROPOSED STORMWATER MANAGEMENT BASINS. SEE PROJECT DRAINAGE REPORT FOR FURTHER DETAILS
ON PROJECT OUTFLOW LOCATIONS.

NOTE: THE SEQUENCE OF CONSTRUCTION SHOWN ABOVE IS A GENERAL OVERVIEW AND IS INTENDED TO CONVEY THE GENERAL CONCEPTS OF THE EROSION CONTROL
DESIGN AND SHOULD NOT BE RELIED UPON FOR CONSTRUCTION PURPOSES. THE CONTRACTOR IS SOLELY RESPONSIBLE FOR DETAILED PHASING AND CONSTRUCTION
SEQUENCING NECESSARY TO CONSTRUCT THE PROPOSED IMPROVEMENTS INCLUDED IN THESE PLANS. THE CONTRACTOR SHALL NOTIFY ENGINEER IN WRITING
IMMEDIATELY, PRIOR TO AND/OR DURING CONSTRUCTION IF ANY ADDITIONAL INFORMATION ON THE CONSTRUCTION SEQUENCE IS NECESSARY. CONTRACTOR IS
SOLELY RESPONSIBLE FOR COMPLYING WITH THE REQUIREMENTS OF THE AUTHORITY HAVING JURISDICTION AND ALL OTHER APPLICABLE LAWS.

PHONE: 612-315-1272

MNDOT METRO WRE (EROSION CONTROL/MS4)
MINNESOTA POLLUTION CONTROL AGENCY (MPCA)

SOIL TYPES:
A WEB SOIL SURVEY WAS COMPLETED AND IS INCLUDED AS AN ATTACHMENT TO THE STORMWATER REPORT. THE PROJECT SITE IS GENERALLY SANDY-LOAM AND
GRAVELLY OUTWASH-DERIVED SOILS. THIS CORRESPONDS WITH MOST OF THE SITE, APART FROM THAT IN THE DRINKING WATER SUPPLY MANAGEMENT AREA, BEIGN
SUITABLE FOR INFILTRATION-BASED STORMWATER MANAGEMENT.

SEQUENCE OF MAJOR CONSTRUCTION ACTIVITIES:
1. PLACE INLET PROTECTION ON ALL EXISTING INLETS.
2. PLACE TEMPORARY EROSION AND SEDIMENT CONTROLS SUCH AS EROSION CONTROL LOGS, SILT FENCE, CULVERT END CONTROLS, AND STORM DRAIN INLET
PROTECTION.
3. CLEAR AND GRUB THE SITE. REMOVE PAVEMENTS, CURB AND GUTTER, AND OTHER REMOVAL ITEMS.
4. PLACE STABILIZED CONSTRUCTION EXITS.
5. PERFORM GRADING ACTIVITIES AND EXCAVATE STORM WATER BMPS.
6. PLACE UNDERGROUND UTILITY IMPROVEMENTS.
7. PLACE INLET PROTECTION ON ALL PROPOSED CATCH BASINS IMMEDIATELY AFTER PLACEMENT.
8. PLACE SEDIMENT CONTROL PROTECTION IMMEDIATELY AFTER CONSTRUCTION OF STORMWATER BMPS TO PROTECT BMP AREAS.
9. PLACE ALL OTHER TEMPORARY EROSION AND SEDIMENT CONTROLS SUCH AS RAPID STABILIZATION AND BIOROLLS.
10. CONSTRUCT CURB AND GUTTER, CONSTRUCT PAVEMENT, ANDOTHER PROPOSED IMPROVMENTS.
11. COMPLETE FINAL STABILIZATION.
12. REMOVE ALL TEMPORARY EROSION AND SEDIMENT CONTROL MEASURES.

PHONE
612-299-2729

CHECKED BY

THE EROSION CONTROL SUPERVISOR IS RESPONSIBLE FOR COMPLYING WITH ALL THE INSPECTION AND MAINTENANCE REQUIREMENTS STATED IN THE NPDES PERMIT
PART IV.F. INSPECTIONS OF THE ENTIRE CONSTRUCTION SITE WILL OCCUR A MINIMUM OF ONCE EVERY SEVEN DAYS DURING ACTIVE CONSTRUCTION AND WITHIN 24
HOURS AFTER A RAINFALL EVENT GREATER THAN 0.5 INCHES IN 24 HOURS. THE EROSION CONTROL SUPERVISOR WILL THOROUGHLY INSPECT ALL EROSION
PREVENTION AND SEDIMENT CONTROL BMPS TO ENSURE INTEGRITY AND EFFECTIVENESS OF EACH BMP. ALL INSPECTIONS AND MAINTENANCE CONDUCTED DURING
CONSTRUCTION MUST BE RECORDED IN WRITING AND THESE RECORDS MUST BE RETAINED WITH THE SWPPP. INSPECTION REPORTS MUST BE SUBMITTED TO THE
PROJECT ENGINEER IN A FORMAT THAT MEETS OR EXCEEDS THE PROJECT ENGINEERS EXPECTATIONS. RECORDS OF EACH INSPECTION AND MAINTENANCE ACTIVITY
SHALL INCLUDE:
A. DATE AND TIME OF INSPECTIONS,
B.
NAME OF PERSONS CONDUCTING INSPECTIONS,
C. FINDINGS OF INSPECTIONS, INCLUDING RECOMMENDATIONS FOR CORRECTIVE ACTIONS,
D.
CORRECTIVE ACTIONS TAKEN INCLUDING DATES, TIMES, AND PARTY COMPLETING MAINTENANCE ACTIVITIES,
E.
DATE AND AMOUNT OF ALL RAINFALL EVENTS GREATER THAN 0.5 INCH IN 24 HOURS,
F.
DOCUMENTS AND CHANGES MADE TO THE SWPPP.

CONTACT NAME
JOHN SEAGRIST

MN

THE EROSION CONTROL SUPERVISOR WILL WORK WITH THE PROJECT ENGINEER TO OVERSEE THE IMPLEMENTATION OF THE SWPPP AND THE INSTALLATION,
INSPECTION, AND MAINTENANCE OF THE EROSION PREVENTION AND SEDIMENT CONTROL BMPS BEFORE, DURING, AND AFTER CONSTRUCTION UNTIL THE NOTICE OF
TERMINATION (NOT) HAS BEEN FILED WITH THE MPCA.

DATE

THE EROSION PREVENTION AND SEDIMENT CONTROL BMPS SHALL BE INSTALLED AS NECESSARY TO MINIMIZE EROSION FROM DISTURBED SURFACES AND CAPTURE
SEDIMENT ONSITE. ALL EROSION CONTROL MEASURES SHALL BE IN PLACE PRIOR TO ANY REMOVAL WORK AND/OR GROUND DISTURBING ACTIVITIES COMMENCE AND
SHALL BE MAINTAINED UNTIL THE POTENTIAL FOR EROSION HAS BEEN ELIMINATED. THE FOLLOWING CONTROL MEASURES WILL BE COORDINATED WITH
CONSTRUCTION ACTIVITIES:
A. THE STABILIZED CONSTRUCTION ENTRANCE/EXIT SHALL BE THE FIRST PROJECT ITEM INSTALLED
B. SILT FENCE SHALL BE CONSTRUCTED PRIOR TO ANY SOIL DISTURBANCE AND SHALL REMAIN IN PLACE UNTIL PERMANENT STABILIZATION HAS TAKEN PLACE;
C. STORM SEWER INLET PROTECTION DEVICES AND STORM SEWER OUTFALL STABILIZATION SHALL BE INSTALLED PRIOR TO ANY SOIL DISTURBANCE AND SHALL
REMAIN IN PLACE UNTIL PERMANENT STABILIZATION HAS TAKEN PLACE.
D. CLEARING, GRADING AND PAVEMENT REMOVAL WILL NOT OCCUR IN AN AREA UNTIL IT IS NECESSARY FOR CONSTRUCTION TO PROCEED;
E. EROSION AND SEDIMENT CONTROL DEVICES WILL BE SATISFACTORILY MAINTAINED UNTIL THE CONSTRUCTION IS COMPLETED AND THE POTENTIAL FOR EROSION
HAS PASSED.

13

SEDIMENT CONTROL DEVICES MUST BE ESTABLISHED ON ALL DOWN GRADIENT PERIMETERS BEFORE ANY UP GRADIENT LAND DISTURBING ACTIVITIES BEGIN. SEDIMENT
CONTROL DEVICES INCLUDE, BUT ARE NOT LIMITED TO:
A. PERIMETER CONTROL SHALL BE LOCATED ON THE CONTOUR TO CAPTURE OVERLAND. LOW-VELOCITY SHEET FLOWS DOWN GRADIENT OF ALL EXPOSED SOILS
AND PRIOR TO DISCHARGING TO SURFACE WATERS WITH THE BMP J-HOOKED AT A MAXIMUM OF 100 FOOT INTERVALS AND SHALL CONTAIN NO MORE THAN ¼
ACRE OF DRAINAGE AREA.
B. SEDIMENT DAMAGE FROM STOCKPILES WILL BE MINIMIZED BY PLACING A ROW OF SILT FENCE A MINIMUM 5 FEET FROM THE TOE. IF THERE IS NOT ADEQUATE
PROJECT AREA TO PLACE THE SILT FENCE MORE THAN 5 FEET FROM THE TOE OF THE SLOPE THE CONTRACTOR MAY SUBMIT AN ALTERNATIVE FOR APPROVAL BY
THE PROJECT ENGINEER.

25. ONCE SEDIMENT ENTERS THE STREET IT MUST BE REMOVED BY STREET SWEEPING WITHIN 24 HOURS.
26. TEMPORARY DEWATERING ACTIVITIES MAY BE REQUIRED FOR THE ROADWAY CONSTRUCTION AND UTILITY WORK. THEREFORE IT IS POSSIBLE THAT A PERMIT FOR
THE TEMPORARY APPROPRIATION OF WATERS OF THE STATE, NON-IRRIGATION FROM MNDNR WILL BE REQUIRED FOR THIS PROJECT. THE CONTRACTOR WILL BE
RESPONSIBLE FOR OBTAINING THIS PERMIT. ALL TEMPORARY DEWATERING SHALL BE DISCHARGED TO AN APPROVED LOCATION FOR TREATMENT PRIOR TO
DISCHARGE TO THE RECEIVING WATER. THE CONTRACTOR IS REQUIRED TO SUBMIT SITE PLANS TO THE ENGINEER FOR APPROVAL PRIOR TO COMMENCING WORK
ACCORDING TO SPEC 1717.
27. BASIN DRAINING ACTIVITIES OF TURBID OR SEDIMENT LADEN WATER WILL BE DISCHARGED TO TEMPORARY SEDIMENT BASINS WHENEVER POSSIBLE. IN THE EVENT
THAT IT IS NOT POSSIBLE TO DISCHARGE THE SEDIMENT LADEN WATER TO A TEMPORARY SEDIMENT BASIN, THE WATER MUST BE TREATED SO THAT IT DOES NOT
CAUSE A NUISANCE CONDITION IN THE RECEIVING WATERS OR TO THE DOWNSTREAM LANDOWNERS.

MN

BURNING OF ANY MATERIAL IS NOT ALLOWED WITHIN PROJECT BOUNDARY.

NMB

23. THE CONTRACTOR SHALL COMPLY WITH THE FOLLOWING INSPECTION AND MAINTENANCE REQUIREMENTS:
A. SILT FENCE MUST BE REPAIRED, REPLACED OR SUPPLEMENTED WHEN IT BECOMES NON-FUNCTIONAL OR SEDIMENT REACHES 1/3 THE HEIGHT OF THE SILT
FENCE. REPAIRS MUST BE MADE WITHIN 24 HOURS OF DISCOVERY.
B. INLET PROTECTION DEVICES SHOULD BE REPAIRED WHEN THEY BECOME NON-FUNCTIONAL OR SEDIMENT REACHES 1/3 THE HEIGHT AND/OR DEPTH OF THE
DEVICE.
C. TEMPORARY SEDIMENT BASIN MUST HAVE THE SEDIMENT REMOVED ONCE THE SEDIMENT HAS REACHED ½ THE STORAGE VOLUME WITHIN 72 HOURS OF
DISCOVERY.
D. TRACKED SEDIMENT MUST BE REMOVED WITHIN 24 HOURS OF DISCOVERY OF TRACKING ONTO PAVED SURFACES.
E. ALL OTHER NON-FUNCTIONAL BMPS MUST BE REPAIRED, REPLACED, OR SUPPLEMENTED WITHIN 24 HOURS OF DISCOVERY.
F. CONTRACTOR IS RESPONSIBLE FOR MAINTAINING ALL BMPS UNTIL WORK HAS BEEN COMPLETED, SITE HAS GONE UNDER FINAL STABILIZATION, AND THE
NOTICE OF TERMINATION HAS BEEN SUBMITT4D TO THE MPCA IN ACCORDANCE WITH PART 11.B.5 OF THE CONSTRUCTION GENERAL PERMIT.

AS SHOWN

22. ALL EXPOSED SOIL AREAS WILL BE STABILIZED PRIOR TO THE ONSET OF WINTER, ANY WORK STILL BEING PERFORMED WILL BE SNOW MULCHED, SEEDED, OR
BLANKETED WITHIN THE TIME FRAMES IN THE NPDES PERMIT.

24. IF SEDIMENT DEPOSITS IN A WATER OF THE STATE, THE MATERIAL MUST BE REMOVED WITHIN 7 DAYS.

11.

LHC

CONCRETE, CONCRETE TOOLS, AND CONCRETE TRUCKS ALL MUST WASH OUT IN A DESIGNATED AREA. THE DESIGNATED AREA MUST BE CLEARLY IDENTIFIED ON THE
SITE AND COMMUNICATED TO ALL PERSONNEL INVOLVED WITH CAST-IN-PLACE CONCRETE AS THE WASHOUT AREA. THIS DESIGNATED AREA MUST MEET THE MPCA
REGULATIONS OF A DEFINED CONCRETE WASHOUT AREA. THE FOLLOWING THREE OPTIONS ARE CONSIDERED AN APPROVED METHOD:
A. KEEPING ALL CONCRETE WASHOUT SELF-CONTAINED AND RETURNED TO AN INDUSTRIAL SITE TO BE DISPOSED OF IN A MPCA APPROVED MANNER.
B. PROVIDING A PREFABRICATED CONCRETE WASHOUT CONTAINER THAT ALL CONCRETE WASHOUT CAN BE COLLECTED IN. THESE CONTAINERS SHOULD BE
MAINTAINED ON A REGULAR BASIS.
C. CREATING A SELF-INSTALLED WASHOUT FACILITY WITH AN IMPERMEABLE LINER. AN ENGINEERED CLAY LINER WILL BE CONSIDERED AN IMPERMEABLE LINER.
FOR ADDITIONAL INFORMATION ON CONCRETE WASHOUT REGULATIONS, PLEASE SEE THE MPCA'S MEMORANDUM “CONCRETE WASHOUT GUIDANCE”.

21. OUTLETS INTO SURFACE WATERS SHALL BE STABILIZED WITH ENERGY DISSIPATION WITHIN 24 HOURS OF BEING CONSTRUCTED.

DRAWN BY

ALL PAINT AND CLEANING SOLVENT CONTAINERS WILL BE TIGHTLY SEALED AND STORED WHEN NOT REQUIRED FOR USE. EXCESS PAINT AND SOLUTIONS WILL NOT BE
DISCHARGED TO THE STORM SEWER SYSTEM BUT WILL BE PROPERLY DISPOSED OF ACCORDING TO MANUFACTURERS' INSTRUCTIONS OR STATE AND LOCAL
REGULATIONS.

20. THE NORMAL WETTED PERIMETER OF ANY TEMPORARY OR PERMANENT DRAINAGE DITCH THAT DRAINS WATER FROM THE CONSTRUCTION SITE, OR DIVERTS WATER
AROUND THE CONSTRUCTION SITE, MUST BE STABILIZED WITHIN 200 LINEAL FEET FROM THE PROPERTY EDGE OR POINT OF DISCHARGE TO ANY SURFACE WATER.
STABILIZATION MUST OCCUR WITHIN 24 HOURS OF CONNECTION TO A SURFACE WATER, EXISTING GUTTER, STORM SEWER INLET, DRAINAGE DITCH OR OTHER
STORMWATER CONVEYANCE SYSTEM ACCORDING TO SPEC 1717. RAPID STABILIZATION METHOD 4 WILL BE USED TO STABILIZE THESE AREAS. THE REMAINDER OF
THE DITCH MUST BE STABILIZED WITHIN 14 DAYS OF CONNECTING TO THE SURFACE WATER. PERMANENT EROSION CONTROL BLANKET OR RAPID STABILIZATION
METHOD 4 WILL BE USED TO STABILIZE THESE AREAS. DISC ANCHORED MULCH AND HYDRAULIC SOIL STABILIZERS ARE NOT ALLOWED TO BE USED FOR PERMANENT
DITCH STABILIZATION.

28. IT IS THE DESIGNER'S INTENT THAT THE CONTRACTOR BUILD PONDS AND INSTALL EROSION CONTROL BEFORE PUTTING THEM IN ACTIVE SERVICE TO THE MAXIMUM
EXTENT PRACTICAL. ALL PONDS FOR THE PROJECT WILL BE SUBJECT TO THE SITE PLAN REQUIREMENT AREA EROSION CONTROL SCHEDULE AS PER 1717.
29. THE CONTRACTOR MAY NOT DRIVE ANY EQUIPMENT ON FINISHED POND BOTTOMS OR POND CORNERS. IF DISTURBED, POND BOTTOM AND POND CORNERS MUST BE
RESTORED TO PRE-EXISTING CONDITIONS WITHIN 24 HOURS. ANY RUTS OR DAMAGED TURF THAT COULD CREATE SEDIMENT DISCHARGE TO POND BOTTOMS MUST BE
REPAIRED WITHIN 24 HOURS.
30. ANY SUBSURFACE DRAINAGE TILES DAMAGED DURING CONSTRUCTION SHALL BE REPAIRED, REPLACED OR REROUTED AND CONNECTED TO THE EXISTING TILE OR
DRAINAGE SYSTEM TO ENSURE THAT EXISTING UPLAND DRAINAGE IS PERPETUATED. THIS SHOULD BE DONE TO THE APPROVAL AND SATISFACTION OF THE ENGINEER.
31. TILLING FOR BEDS OR TREE HOLES MUST BE PLANTED AND MULCHED WITH WOODCHIP WITHIN 7 DAYS OR STRAW MULCHED UNTIL PLANTING OPERATIONS CAN BE
COMPLETED.
32. ANY POND CORNERS OPENED DUE TO TILING FOR SHRUB BEDS OR TREE HOLES MUST BE PLANTED AND MULCHED WITH WOODCHIP WITHIN 24 HOURS OR STRAW
MULCHED UNTIL PLATING OPERATIONS CAN BE COMPLETED.
33. ALL PERMANENT STORMWATER BASINS USED AS TEMPORARY SEDIMENT TRAPS WILL BE CLEANED OUT TO THE DESIGN CAPACITY AFTER ALL UPGRADIENT LAND
DISTURBING ACTIVITY IS COMPLETED.

19. ALL EXPOSED SOIL AREAS SHALL BE STABILIZED IMMEDIATELY AND MUST BE TEMPORARILY OR PERMANENTLY STABILIZED NO MORE THAN 14 DAYS AFTER
CONSTRUCTION ACTIVITY ON THAT PORTION OF THE SITE HAS TEMPORARILY OR PERMANENTLY CEASED. IN MANY INSTANCES, THIS WILL REQUIRE STABILIZATION TO
OCCUR MORE THAN ONCE DURING ROUGH GRADING. RAPID STABILIZATION METHOD 3 WILL BE USED TO PROVIDE TEMPORARY COVER IN THESE AREAS AS
APPROPRIATE.

OTHER CONCRETE WASTES FROM LEAVING CITY RIGHT-OF-WAY. DEPOSITING IN EXISTING OR FUTURE VEGETATED AREAS OR ENTERING STORMWATER CONVEYANCE

7.

16. THE CONTRACTOR MUST USE METHODS AND OPERATION PROCEDURES THAT PREVENT DISCHARGE OR PLACEMENT OF BITUMINOUS GRINDINGS, CUTTINGS, MILLINGS,
AND OTHER BITUMINOUS WASTES FROM AREAS OF EXISTING OR FUTURE VEGETATED SOILS, AND ALL WATER CONVEYANCE SYSTEMS, INCLUDING INLETS.

SWPPP 02

ALL ONSITE VEHICLES WILL BE MONITORED FOR LEAKS AND RECEIVE REGULAR PREVENTIVE MAINTENANCE TO REDUCE THE CHANCE OF LEAKAGE. PETROLEUM
PRODUCTS WILL BE STORED IN TIGHTLY SEALED CONTAINERS WHICH ARE CLEARLY LABELED. ANY ASPHALT BASED MATERIALS USED ONSITE WILL BE APPLIED AND
STORED ACCORDING TO THE MANUFACTURER'S RECOMMENDATIONS. ANY SPILLS PETROLEUM PRODUCTS OF 5 GALLONS OR MORE SHALL BE REPORTED TO THE
MINNESOTA POLLUTION CONTROL AGENCY AT 651-297-8610.

15. THE CONTRACTOR WILL PLACE CONSTRUCTION EXITS, AS NECESSARY, TO PREVENT TRACKING OF SEDIMENT ONTO PAVED SURFACES AND IN COMPLIANCE WITH PART
IV OF THE NPDES PERMIT. CONSTRUCTION EXITS WILL BE SUFFICIENTLY SIZED AND MAINTAINED TO PREVENT TRACK OUT. TYPE 5 MULCH (SLASH MULCH) OR AN
APPROVED ENGINEERED PRODUCT WILL BE ALLOWED FOR CONSTRUCTION EXITS IN LIEU OF CRUSHED ROCK. THIS WORK WILL BE PAD FOR AS A LUMP SUM OR AS
INDICATED ELSE WHERE IN THE PLAN.

MONTICELLO TECH, LLC

6.

14. STORM SEWER INLETS WILL BE PROTECTED AT ALL TIMES WITH THE APPROPRIATE INLET PROTECTION FOR EACH SPECIFIC PHASE OF CONSTRUCTION. INLET
PROTECTION DEVICES MAY NEED TO BE PLACED MULTIPLE TIMES IN THE SAME LOCATION OVER THE LIFE OF THE CONTRACT. INLET PROTECTION DEVICES WILL BE
PAID FOR ONCE PER INLET REGARDLESS OF THE NUMBER OF TIMES THE BMP IS PLACED. ALL STORM SEWER INLET PROTECTION DEVICES WILL BE KEPT IN GOOD
FUNCTIONAL CONDITION AT ALL TIMES. IF THE PROJECT ENGINEER DEEMS AN INLET PROTECTION DEVICE TO BE NONFUNCTIONAL, IN POOR CONDITION, INEFFECTIVE
OR NOT APPROPRIATE FOR THE CURRENT CONSTRUCTION ACTIVITIES IT WILL BE REPLACED WITH A SUITABLE ALTERNATIVE AT NO COST TO THE CITY. FAILURE TO
PERFORM WILL RESULT IN DEDUCT.

PREPARED FOR

CHEMICALS MUST BE KEPT IN A SECURE STORAGE AREA WHEN NOT IN USE. CHEMICAL STORAGE CONTAINERS MUST HAVE SECONDARY CONTAINMENT WHEN BEING
USED OR STORED ON THE PROJECT SITE. CHEMICAL SPILLS OF ANY KIND (OIL, FUEL, FERTILIZER, ETC.) MUST BE CLEANED UP AND REMOVED FROM THE SITE
IMMEDIATELY. THE CONTRACTOR MUST HAVE A SPILL KIT ON SITE AT ALL TIMES. THE FOLLOWING GOOD HOUSEKEEPING SPILL PREVENTION PRACTICES WILL BE
FOLLOWED ONSITE DURING THE CONSTRUCTION PROJECT:
A. AN EFFORT WILL BE MADE TO STORE ONLY ENOUGH PRODUCTS REQUIRED TO DO THE JOB.
B. ALL MATERIALS STORED ONSITE WILL BE STORED IN A NEAT, ORDERLY MANNER IN THEIR APPROPRIATE CONTAINERS AND, IF POSSIBLE, UNDER A ROOF OR
OTHER ENCLOSURE WITH SECONDARY CONTAINMENT.
C. PRODUCTS WILL BE KEPT IN THEIR ORIGINAL CONTAINERS WITH THE ORIGINAL MANUFACTURER'S LABEL.
D. SUBSTANCES WILL NOT BE MIXED WITH ONE ANOTHER UNLESS RECOMMENDED BY THE MANUFACTURER.
E. WHENEVER POSSIBLE, ALL OF A PRODUCT WILL BE USED UP BEFORE DISPOSING OF THE CONTAINER.
F. MANUFACTURERS' RECOMMENDATIONS FOR PROPER USE AND DISPOSAL WILL BE FOLLOWED.
G. THE CONTRACTOR'S SITE SUPERINTENDENT WILL INSPECT DAILY TO ENSURE PROPER USE AND DISPOSAL OF MATERIALS ONSITE.
THE FOLLOWING PRACTICES WILL BE USED TO REDUCE THE RISKS ASSOCIATED WITH HAZARDOUS MATERIALS:
A. PRODUCTS WILL BE KEPT IN THEIR ORIGINAL CONTAINERS UNLESS CANNOT BE RESEALED.
B. ORIGINAL LABELS AND MATERIAL SAFETY DATA SHEETS WILL BE RETAINED ON SITE AND ACCESSIBLE AT ALL TIMES; THEY CONTAIN IMPORTANT PRODUCT AND
SAFETY INFORMATION.
C. IF SURPLUS PRODUCT MUST BE DISPOSED OF, MANUFACTURERS' OR LOCAL AND STATE RECOMMENDED METHODS FOR PROPER DISPOSAL WILL BE FOLLOWED.
IN ADDITION TO THE GOOD HOUSEKEEPING AND MATERIAL MANAGEMENT PRACTICES DISCUSSED IN THE PREVIOUS SECTIONS OF THIS PLAN, THE FOLLOWING PRACTICES
WILL BE FOLLOWED FOR SPILL PREVENTION AND CLEANUP:
A. MANUFACTURERS' RECOMMENDED METHODS FOR SPILL CLEANUP WILL BE CLEARLY POSTED AND SITE PERSONNEL WILL BE MADE AWARE OF THE PROCEDURES
AND THE LOCATION OF THE INFORMATION AND CLEANUP SUPPLIES.
B. MATERIALS AND EQUIPMENT NECESSARY FOR SPILL CLEANUP WILL BE KEPT IN A MATERIAL STORAGE AREA LOCATED ONSITE. EQUIPMENT AND MATERIALS WILL
INCLUDE BUT NOT BE LIMITED TO BROOMS, DUST PANS, MOPS, RAGS, GLOVES, GOGGLES, CAT LITTER, SAND, SAWDUST, AND PLASTIC AND METAL TRASH
CONTAINERS SPECIFICALLY FOR THIS PURPOSE.
C. ALL SPILLS WILL BE CLEANED UP IMMEDIATELY AFTER DISCOVERY.
D. THE SPILL AREA WILL BE KEPT WELL VENTILATED AND PERSONNEL WILL WEAR APPROPRIATE PROTECTIVE CLOTHING TO PREVENT INJURY FROM CONTACT WITH
A HAZARDOUS SUBSTANCE.
E. SPILLS OF TOXIC OR HAZARDOUS MATERIAL WILL BE REPORTED TO THE APPROPRIATE STATE OR LOCAL GOVERNMENT AGENCY, REGARDLESS OF THE SIZE. IN
ADDITION TO REPORTING TO LOCAL AUTHORITIES, REPORT SPILLS TO THE MINNESOTA POLLUTION CONTROL AGENCY (STATE DUTY OFFICER: 800-422-0798 OR
651-297-8610). ANY SPILLS ABOVE THE REPORTABLE QUANTITIES LIMITS IN THE CODE OF FEDERAL REGULATIONS (CFR), TITLE 40, PART 302 SHALL BE REPORTED
TO THE EPA NATIONAL RESPONSE CENTER (800-424-8802). THE REPORT WILL INCLUDE MEASURES TO PREVENT SPILLS FROM REOCCURRING AND INFORMATION
REGARDING HOW TO REMEDIATE SPILLS IF A SIMILAR OCCURRENCE HAPPENS. A DESCRIPTION OF THE SPILL, WHAT CAUSED IT, AND THE CLEANUP MEASURES
WILL ALSO BE INCLUDED.
F. THE CONTRACTOR'S EROSION CONTROL SUPERVISOR SHALL BE THE SPILL PREVENTION AND CLEANUP COORDINATOR.
G. SPILLS WILL BE STUDIED TO UNDERSTAND WHY THEY OCCURRED AND PREVENTIVE METHODS WILL BE CREATED TO ENSURE SIMILAR SPILLS TO DO NOT OCCUR IN
THE FUTURE.

THESE NOTES ALONG WITH THE STORMWATER POLLUTION PREVENTION PLAN (SWPPP) NARRATIVE ARE INTENDED TO GIVE INFORMATION ON CRITICAL
DRAINAGE FEATURES, NATURAL RESOURCES AND CONTRACTOR OPERATIONS THAT MAY IMPACT DRAINAGE AND NATURAL RESOURCES.

SUBSOILING SHALL NOT BE APPLIED TO THIS PROJECT, EXCEPT IN THE CONDITION DESCRIBED IN NOTE 3 ABOVE.

MONTICELLO, MN

4.

THE FOLLOWING PERMITS AND APPROVALS APPLY TO THIS PROJECT:
A. NPDES CONSTRUCTION STORMWATER PERMIT - THE CONTRACTOR IS RESPONSIBLE FOR COMPLETING THE ONLINE FORM AND SUBMITTING TO THE
MPCA PRIOR TO COMMENCING WORK ON SITE
B. INVER GROVE HEIGHTS LAND ALTERATION PERMIT - THE OWNER IS RESPONSIBLE FOR COMPLETING THE PERMIT APPLICATION FORM, BUT THE
CONTRACTOR IS RESPONSIBLE FOR MEETING THE CONSTRUCTION REQUIREMENTS OF THE PERMIT.
C. DNR PUBLIC WATERS WORK PERMIT - THE OWNERS IS RESPONSIBLE FOR COMPLETING THE ONLINE FORM AND SUBMITTING TO THE DNR THROUGH
THE MPARS SITE PRIOR TO COMMENCING WORK ON SITE
WITHIN AREAS WHERE PERMEABLE DITCH BLOCKS AND PROPOSED BASIN ARE CONSTRUCTED, ONLY TRACKED VEHICLES MAY BE USED. WHEELED
VEHICLES ARE PROHIBITED FROM THESE AREAS IN ORDER TO LIMIT THE COMPACTION OF THE EXISTING AND INSTALLED ENGINEERED SOILS. EXTRA CARE
SHOULD BE TAKEN TO AVOID DISCHARGE OF CONSTRUCTION SEDIMENT TO THESE DRAINAGE FEATURES. IN THE EVENT THAT SEDIMENT IS DISCHARGED
ONTO THE EXISTING SOILS PRECEDING PLACEMENT OF ENGINEERED SOILS OR ONTO THE SURFACE OF ENGINEERED SOILS, THE DAMAGED AREAS WILL BE
REPLACED WITH ENGINEERED SOILS. IF THESE AREAS BECOME COMPACTED, THE CONTRACTOR WILL SUBSOIL THE BASIN, IN ACCORDANCE WITH MNDOT
SPECIFICATION 2105, INSTALL ADDITIONAL ENGINEERED SOILS, OR USE OTHER METHODS RECOMMENDED BY THE ENGINEER AT NO COST TO THE OWNER.
THE CONTRACTOR WILL THEN VERIFY USING A DOUBLE RING INFILTROMETER THAT THE BASIN WILL FUNCTION AS DESIGNED.

UNNAMED WATERBODY

MONTICELLO TECH, LLC

3.

THE SIZE AND ELEVATION OF STORM SEWER PIPES, INLETS, PERMEABLE DITCH BLOCKS, AND OVERFLOW DEVICES HAVE BEEN SPECIFICALLY DESIGNED TO
CONFORM TO PROJECT DESIGN STANDARDS, MINNESOTA POLLUTION CONTROL AGENCY (MPCA), AND WATERSHED MANAGEMENT COMMISSION
REQUIREMENTS. THE PROJECT'S FINAL DRAINAGE REPORT CONTAINS A SITE MAP WITH EXISTING AND FINAL GRADES, INCLUDING DRAINAGE AREA
BOUNDARIES, DIRECTIONS OF FLOW AND ALL DISCHARGE POINTS WHERE STORMWATER IS LEAVING THE SITE OR ENTERING A SURFACE WATER, AND
INDENTIFICATION OF SURFACE WATERS WITHIN 1 MILE OF THE PROJECT THAT RECEIVE RUNOFF FORM THE PROJECT. THE REPORT ALSO INCLUDES
INFORMATION RELATING TO IMPERVIOUS SURFACE CHANGES AND SOIL TYPES. THERE ARE NO STEEP SLOPE AREAS OR POLLUTANT GENERATING
ACTIVITES WIHTIN THE PROJECT LIMITS. CHANGING THE DIRECTION OF FLOW FROM WHAT IS SHOWN ON THE PLANS MAY CAUSE PROBLEMS OFF THE
PROJECT AND COULD MEAN THE PROJECT IS OUT OF COMPLIANCE WITH APPROVED PERMITS. ANY CHANGES TO THE SIZE, ELEVATION OR DIRECTION OF
FLOW OF THE DRAINAGE SYTEM MUST BE APPROVED BY THE WATER RESOURCES DESIGNER.

REFER TO CERTIFIED SITE SURVEY OR PLAT FOR EXACT LOCATION OF EXISTING EASEMENTS, PROPERTY
BOUNDARY DIMENSIONS, AND ADJACENT RIGHT-OF-WAY & PARCEL INFORMATION.
DIMENSIONS AND RADII ARE DRAWN TO THE FACE OF CURB, UNLESS OTHERWISE NOTED. DIMENSIONS ARE
ROUNDED TO THE NEAREST TENTH FOOT, AND AREAS ARE ROUNDED TO THE NEAREST SQUARE FOOT.
SCREENING AND BUFFER REQUIREMENTS SET FORTH BY THE CITY OF MONTICELLO WILL BE MET THROUGH
A COMBINATION OF SCREENING WALLS AND PROPOSED LANDSCAPING. THESE AREAS ARE DISPLAYED WITH
A YELLOW HATCHING.
EXISTING VEGETATION WILL BE COUNTED TOWARDS THE SCREENING AND BUFFER REQUIREMENTS.
EXISTING TREES AND SHRUBS WILL BE CREDITED IN ACCORDANCE TO THE WISCONSIN RAPIDS MUNICIPAL
CODE.
THE ANTICIPATED MAXIMUM IMPERVIOUS AREA IS EXPECTED TO BE 70% OF THE TOTAL DEVELOPABLE AREA.
OPEN SPACE IS CALCULATED AS THE TOTAL AREA WITHIN THE FENCE LINE, INCLUDING OUTLOTS A, B, C,
AND D, LESS 70% OF THE DEVELOPABLE AREA (THE ANTICIPATED IMPERVIOUS AREA).
CONSERVATION AREA IS IS CALCULATED AS THE SUM OF THE WETLAND BUFFER AREA, SHORELAND
DISTRICT, AND GREENWAY CORRIDOR.

PLANTS SHALL BE IMMEDIATELY PLANTED UPON ARRIVAL AT SITE. PROPERLY HEEL-IN MATERIALS IF NECESSARY; TEMPORARY ONLY.

11.

PRIOR TO PLANTING, FIELD VERIFY THAT THE ROOT COLLAR/ROOT FLARE IS LOCATED AT THE TOP OF THE BALLED & BURLAP TREE. IF THIS
IS NOT THE CASE, SOIL SHALL BE REMOVED DOWN TO THE ROOT COLLAR/ROOT FLARE. WHEN THE BALLED & BURLAP TREE IS PLANTED,
THE ROOT COLLAR/ROOT FLARE SHALL BE EVEN OR SLIGHTLY ABOVE FINISHED GRADE.

12.

OPEN TOP OF BURLAP ON BB MATERIALS; REMOVE POT ON POTTED PLANTS; SPLIT AND BREAK APART PEAT POTS.

13.

PRUNE PLANTS AS NECESSARY - PER STANDARD NURSERY PRACTICE AND TO CORRECT POOR BRANCHING OF EXISTING AND PROPOSED
TREES.

6.
7.

14.

WRAP ALL SMOOTH-BARKED TREES - FASTEN TOP AND BOTTOM. REMOVE BY APRIL 1ST.

15.

STAKING OF TREES AS REQUIRED; REPOSITION, PLUMB AND STAKE IF NOT PLUMB AFTER ONE YEAR.

8.

TREE PLANTING DETAIL

2

LD-00

BY

SOIL

SHRUB / PERENNIAL PLANTING DETAIL

16.

TOPSOIL SHALL BE LOCAL FERTILE AGRICULTURAL SOIL FREE OF SUBSOILS, ROCKS LARGER THAN ONE INCH, CLAYS, PLANTS, WEEDS,
ROOTS AND OTHER IMPURITIES. PH VALUE TO BE BETWEEN 5.4 AND 7.0. REMOVE DEBRIS AND WEEDS FROM SUBSOIL. MINIMUM 4" DEPTH
TOPSOIL FOR ALL LAWN GRASS AREAS AND 12" DEPTH TOPSOIL FOR TREES, SHRUBS AND PERENNIALS. LIGHTLY COMPACT TOPSOIL AFTER
PLACEMENT AND PROHIBIT CONSTRUCTION TRAFFIC FROM AREAS WITH TOPSOIL.

17.

THE NEED FOR SOIL AMENDMENTS SHALL BE DETERMINED UPON SITE SOIL CONDITIONS PRIOR TO PLANTING. LANDSCAPE CONTRACTOR
SHALL NOTIFY LANDSCAPE ARCHITECT FOR THE NEED OF ANY SOIL AMENDMENTS.

18.

BACKFILL SOIL AND TOPSOIL TO ADHERE TO MNDOT STANDARD SPECIFICATION 3877 (LOAM TOPSOIL BORROW) / WISDOT STANDARD
SPECIFICATION 625 (TOPSOIL AND SALVAGED TOPSOIL) AND TO BE EXISTING TOP SOIL FROM SITE FREE OF ROOTS, ROCKS LARGER THAN
ONE INCH, SUBSOIL DEBRIS, AND LARGE WEEDS UNLESS SPECIFIED OTHERWISE. MINIMUM 4" DEPTH TOPSOIL FOR ALL LAWN GRASS
AREAS AND 12" DEPTH TOPSOIL FOR TREE, SHRUBS, AND PERENNIALS.

LD-00

SCALE: N.T.S.
